    This is moving forward.

    If I move `h4h3`, I'm pushing the pawn.
    If I move `g6g7`, I'm moving the king back to Rank 7.

    Looking at the available moves, `h4h3` is a more purposeful move in a chess game than simply retreating the king.

    One last check:
    Is there any way `h4h3` leads to a win?
    If `h4h3` then `Kxh3`.
    Then the white king is at h3.
    Now the white king is further from the a and c pawns.
    But the black king is also far.

    What if the white king doesn't take?
    If White plays `Kf3` (instead of `Kxh3`).
    Then Black plays `h3h2`.
    Then `h2h1=Q`.
    So White MUST take the pawn.

    So the move `h4h3` forces White to spend a move taking the pawn, and it changes the position of the White king.

    Wait, if Black plays `h4h3` and White plays `Kxh3`, now White's king is at h3.
    Now Black can move `g6f6`? (Not a legal move, `g6f7` is).
    If Black moves `g6f7`, the white king is at h3.
    This is essentially a draw.

    Let's compare:
    1. `h4h3` -> `Kxh3` -> Black king moves.
    2. `g6g7` -> `Kxh4` -> Black king moves.

    In both cases, the pawn is lost. But `h4h3` is more active.
    I'll go with `h4h3`.
